\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+2\mathcal{H}^{\prime}T^{2}n^{\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}n^{\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}T_{,\beta}n^{\alpha}\bigg\}\bigg\rvert^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}\,,

while for the integral we derive

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\,\left({\widetilde{\delta}\hat{\Gamma}}{}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{\delta}\nu\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\prime\alpha}+\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\beta}\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}=

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\,\left(\delta\hat{\Gamma}^{\alpha}-\delta\nu{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}-\bigg\{\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}T\left(n^{\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-T\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}-2\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}n^{\alpha}+\left(2\mathcal{H}^{2}-\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}n^{\alpha}-\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\left({\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+2\mathcal{H}T_{,\beta}n^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-\mathcal{H}_{o}T_{o}\left({\delta n}^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)-T{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}\delta\nu+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}-2\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_{o}TT_{o}n^{\alpha}\bigg\}\bigg\rvert^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}\,.

(4.35)

The substitutions employed to obtain these equations are the same mentioned in the previous case of
\delta\nu
, and we do not repeat them here.
The sum of these two contributions leads, after straightforward simplifications, to

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{x_{s}})-{\widetilde{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{x_{o}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}(x_{s})-{{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}(x_{o})+\bigg\{\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}Tn^{\alpha}+\Delta x^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(2\mathcal{H}Tn^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)

(4.36)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}\delta\nu+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}{\delta n}^{\beta}+\left(2\mathcal{H}T-\mathcal{H}_{o}T_{o}\right)\left({\delta n}^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)-2\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_{o}TT_{o}n^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\left(2\mathcal{H}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}n^{\alpha}\bigg\}\bigg\rvert^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}\,.

As before, the expectation for this equation can be read from Eq. (
4.25
), and we find no discrepancy once the following second-order relations are taken into account

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\bigg\rvert_{x}=\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}}+\Delta x^{\mu}\left[\partial_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)\right]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}}\,,\qquad(\mathcal{H}T)(x)=\mathcal{H}(\bar{\eta})T(\bar{x})+\Delta x^{\mu}[\partial_{\mu}\left(\mathcal{H}T\right)]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}}\,.\qquad

(4.37)

4.4
Fluctuations in the observed redshift

The observed redshift
z(x_{s};x_{o})
is a cosmological observable, and as such, does not depend on the choice of FLRW coordinates we use to describe it. Geometrically, this means that
z
is a scalar under diffeomorphisms of
\mathcal{M}
.
We will make use of this fact to compute the gauge transformation of the perturbation
\delta z(x_{s};x_{o})
, which again is not a tensor. As discussed in the previous subsection, we will suppress the dependence of
z
and
\delta z
from the reference position
x^{\mu}_{o}
, since the evaluation at the observer position
O
does not participate in gauge transformations.

\bullet

Second-order expectation
.—
We recall the transformation law of a scalar under change of coordinates in Eq. (
2.89
), therefore the observed redshift satisfies

\widetilde{z}(\widetilde{x}_{s})=z(x_{s})\,,

(4.38)

where the reference coordinates in the left-hand side are
\widetilde{x}^{\mu}_{o}
, and in the right-hand side
x^{\mu}_{o}
. Using our definition for the splitting in Eq. (
2.52
)
we can recast the previous equation in terms of
\delta z
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta z}(\widetilde{x})=\frac{a(\widetilde{\eta})}{a(\eta)}\left(1+\delta z(x)\right)-1\,,

(4.39)

where we suppressed the subscript
s
. This expression is valid at any perturbative order, and we can obtain a second-order expression expanding
\widetilde{x}^{\mu}
in Eq. (
2.91
) around
x^{\mu}
. The expansion of the scale factor is

\displaystyle a(\widetilde{\eta}=\eta+T)=a(\eta)\left\{1+\mathcal{H}T+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{H}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime})T^{2}+\cdots\right\}.

(4.40)

Substituting this result in Eq. (
4.39
) we obtain at second order

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta z}(\widetilde{x})=\delta z+\mathcal{H}T(1+\delta z)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}\,,

(4.41)

which is the expression that we will need for the consistency check. Combining the three equations above, we derive the second-order gauge transformation of
\delta z
:

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\delta z}+\mathcal{H}T(1+\delta z)-\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}-\mathcal{H}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}T_{,\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}-\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}-T\delta z^{\prime}-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\delta z_{,\alpha}\,,

(4.42)

where all the quantities are evaluated at the coordinate value of the source position
x^{\mu}_{s}
, and the dependence on the observer reference position is only implicit.

\bullet

Second-order direct computation
.—
To compute the gauge transformation of our second-order expression of the distortion in the observed redshift given in Eq. (
3.42
), we need to compute the gauge transformations of the perturbations
\delta\eta_{o}
and
\widehat{\Delta\nu}
. The perturbation
\delta\eta_{o}
is related to the time lapse at the observer position via Eq. (
3.28
), and we have already checked the consistency of our second-order expression for the time lapse at the observer position. We only need to check the gauge transformation of
\widehat{\Delta\nu}
directly from Eq. (
3.43
). Note that this expression contains perturbations of the conformal wave vector and perturbations of the metric. Since we have already checked the consistency of perturbations in the conformal wave vector, we can use the results of the previous subsection, together with the results in Subsection
4.1
, to derive the second-order gauge transformation

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\widehat{\Delta\nu}}}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta\nu}+\left(\mathcal{H}T-\mathcal{H}_{o}T_{o}\right)(1+\widehat{\Delta\nu})+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}-\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}_{o}^{2}-\mathcal{H}_{o}^{\prime}\right)T_{o}^{2}-\widehat{\Delta\nu}^{\prime}T

(4.43)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\left(\widehat{\Delta\nu}_{,\alpha}+\mathcal{H}T_{,\alpha}\right)-\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_{o}TT_{o}-\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}\,.

This expression is evaluated at
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
as in Eq. (
3.43
) with the reference observer position
O
. For consistency, we expand the second-order fields around the background observer position
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{\rm o}
via Eq. (
3.10
):

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}T)(x_{o})=\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}T_{\bar{\rm o}}+\delta x^{\mu}_{o}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}(\mathcal{H}T)\right]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}.

(4.44)

Substituting the previous two equations in the expression for the redshift perturbation, together with the transformation of
\delta\eta_{o}
in Eq. (
4.14
), we derive the second-order

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}z(\widetilde{x})=\delta z(x)+\mathcal{H}_{z}T_{z}(1+\delta z)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\left[{\partial\over\partial x^{\mu}}\left(\mathcal{H}T\right)\right]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,

(4.45)

where the subscript
z
indicates the evaluation of the second-order fields at
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
.
Again, the fields in the expectation in Eq. (
4.41
) are evaluated at the source position
x^{\mu}_{s}
, while the expression above is evaluated at
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
. Once we expand
(\mathcal{H}T)(x_{s})
around
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
as in Eq. (
4.44
) we recover the consistency between the expectation and direct computation
[
92
]
.

4.5
Distortion in the source position compared to the observed position

Here we will compute the transformation of the distortion in the source position around the background
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
parameterized by the observed redshift and angles. The computations will be performed both in rectangular coordinates
\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}
and in spherical coordinates
\delta r\,,\,\delta\theta\,,\,\delta\phi
. The expression in spherical coordinates around observable quantities will be needed to compute the gauge transformations in the next subsection.

\bullet

Second-order expectation
.—
The expectation for the second-order transformation of
\Delta x_{s}^{\mu}
has already been derived in Eq. (
4.8
), here we write the components explicitly:

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}\eta}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta\eta_{s}+T(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta\eta_{s}T^{\prime}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}+\Delta x^{\alpha}_{s}T{}_{,\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}x}^{\alpha}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta x^{\alpha}_{s}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta\eta_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}+\Delta x^{\beta}_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,.

(4.46)

With the transformation of the spatial distortion
\Delta x_{s}^{\alpha}
, we can derive the transformation laws satisfied by the deviations in spherical coordinates. By direct substitution, we obtain

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta}r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{\alpha}{\widetilde{\Delta}x}{}^{\alpha}_{s}+\frac{\bar{r}_{z}}{2}\left(\widetilde{\delta}\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta~\widetilde{\delta}\phi^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\delta r}+\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}+\frac{1}{2\bar{r}_{z}}\left(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\theta}+\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\phi}\right)+\mathcal{L}_{\theta}\,\delta\theta+\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\sin\theta\,\delta\phi\,,

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{z}{\widetilde{\delta}\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{\alpha}{\widetilde{\Delta}x}{}^{\alpha}_{s}-\widetilde{\delta}r\,\widetilde{\delta}\theta+\frac{\bar{r}_{z}}{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta~\widetilde{\delta}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{z}{\delta\theta}+\mathcal{L}_{\theta}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}-\frac{\delta r}{\bar{r}_{z}}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}-\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}\left(\delta\theta+\frac{\mathcal{L}_{\theta}}{\bar{r}_{z}}\right)+\frac{1}{2\bar{r}_{z}}\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\phi}\cot{\theta}+\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\cos\theta\,\delta\phi\,,

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,{\widetilde{\delta}\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{\alpha}{\widetilde{\Delta}x}{}^{\alpha}_{s}-\sin\theta~\widetilde{\delta}r\,\widetilde{\delta}\phi-\bar{r}_{z}\cos\theta~\widetilde{\delta}\theta\,\widetilde{\delta}\phi

(4.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,{\delta\phi}+\mathcal{L}_{\phi}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{L}_{\phi}-\frac{\delta r}{\bar{r}_{z}}\mathcal{L}_{\phi}-\frac{\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}}{\bar{r}_{z}}\left(\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,\delta\phi+\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\right)-\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\cot\theta\,\delta\theta

\displaystyle-{\mathcal{L}_{\theta}\over\bar{r}_{z}}\left(\bar{r}_{z}\cos\theta\,\delta\phi-\cot\theta\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\right)\,,

where we defined
\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}:=\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}n_{\alpha}\,
,
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}:=\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\theta_{\alpha}\,
,
\mathcal{L}_{\phi}:=\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\phi_{\alpha}\,
and expressed each field in the right-hand side at the reference position
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
.
Note that, once we have verified the consistency of our second-order expressions for
\Delta\eta_{s}
and
\Delta x_{s}^{\alpha}
in Eqs. (
3.51
) and (
3.54
) with the expectations in Eq. (
4.5
), then the consistency of our second-order expressions for the distortion in spherical coordinates in Eq. (
3.6
) is automatically recovered because
\delta r,\delta\theta,\delta\phi
are simply reparameterizations of
\Delta x^{\alpha}_{s}
.

\bullet

Second-order direct computation
.—
We start by checking the consistency of
\Delta\eta_{s}
given in Eq. (
3.51
). Our second-order expression depends only on
\delta z
, of which we verified the consistency in the previous subsection. We can therefore substitute Eq. (
4.41
) for the gauge transformation of
\delta z
. After straightforward manipulations, we recover the expected result

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}\eta}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta\eta_{s}+T(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta\eta_{s}T^{\prime}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}+\Delta x^{\alpha}_{s}T{}_{,\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,.

(4.48)

We now turn our attention to the spatial components
\Delta x_{s}^{\alpha}
of the distortion in the source position. From Eq. (
3.53
), we need to compute the gauge transformations of boundary terms and line of sight integration. To avoid cumbersome equations, we present the terms separately. For the boundary terms, we derive

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta x}}{}^{\alpha}_{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta x^{\alpha}_{o}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}_{\bar{\rm o}}+\delta x^{\mu}_{o}(\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha})\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}\,,

(4.49)

\displaystyle-n^{\alpha}({\widetilde{\Delta}\eta}_{s}-{\widetilde{\delta\eta}}_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-n^{\alpha}\bigg({\Delta\eta}_{s}-{{\delta\eta}}_{o}+T_{z}-T_{\bar{\rm o}}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}(\partial_{\mu}T)\big\rvert_{z}-\delta x^{\mu}_{o}(\partial_{\mu}T)\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}\bigg)\,,\quad

(4.50)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}x^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(\widetilde{\Delta}x{}^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\widetilde{\Delta}\eta\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta x^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(\Delta x{}^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}T\right)+T\big(\Delta x^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta^{\prime}

(4.51)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}T^{\prime}\big)+\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\big(\Delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}T_{,\beta}\big)\,.\qquad\,\,\,

To compute the transformations of the distortion in the observer and source position,
\delta x^{\mu}_{o}
and
\Delta\eta_{s}
, we used Eq. (
4.8
) as we already verified the consistency of these expressions. From the results of previous subsections, we can readily see that also the transformation of the linear-order perturbation
\Delta x_{s}^{\alpha}
in Eq. (
3.45
) satisfies the linear-order expectation in Eq. (
4.8
).
The gauge transformation of the integral yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\left[{\widetilde{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}-n^{\alpha}{\widetilde{\delta}\nu}+\widetilde{\delta}\nu\left(\widetilde{\Delta}x{}^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\widetilde{\Delta}\eta{}^{\prime}\right)-\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\beta}\left(\widetilde{\Delta}x{}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\widetilde{\Delta}\eta_{,\beta}\right)\right]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\bigg\{{{\delta n}}{}^{\alpha}-n^{\alpha}{\delta\nu}+\delta\nu\left(\Delta x{}^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta{}^{\prime}\right)-{\delta n}^{\beta}\left(\Delta x{}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}\right)+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\bigg[\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}T

\displaystyle-T\big(\Delta x^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta^{\prime}+\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}T^{\prime}\big)-\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\big(\Delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}T_{,\beta}\big)\bigg]\bigg\}\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\,,\quad

(4.52)

where for the gauge transformations of the components of the conformal wave vector we used Eqs. (
4.27
) and (
4.28
) since we have already checked the consistency of our second-order expressions.
Summing the contributions of the boundary terms and the integral, most of the terms cancel, and we recover

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\Delta}x}{}^{\alpha}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta x^{\alpha}_{s}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta\eta_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}+\Delta x^{\beta}_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,

(4.53)

in agreement with the expectation.

4.6
Distortion in the physical volume and area

\bullet

Second-order expectation
.—
In Subsection
2.9
we showed that the fluctuations
\delta V
and
\delta A
in Eqs. (
2.80
) and (
2.83
) are invariant under diffeomorphisms of
\mathcal{M}
, which is consistent with the requirement that they are observed quantities. Consequently, the following relations hold under a change of coordinates

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta V}(\widetilde{x}_{s})=\delta V(x_{s})\,,\qquad\qquad\widetilde{\delta A}(\widetilde{x}_{s})=\delta A(x_{s})\,,

(4.54)

i.e. invariant under diffeomorphism
[
110
,
103
]
.
These are valid at any perturbative order, while the second-order gauge transformations can be read from Eq. (
2.89
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta V}(\bar{x}_{z})=\delta V(\bar{x}_{z})-T\delta V^{\prime}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\delta V_{,\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,\quad\widetilde{\delta A}(\bar{x}_{z})=\delta A(\bar{x}_{z})-T\delta A^{\prime}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\delta A_{,\alpha}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,\,\,\,\,\quad

(4.55)

\bullet

Second-order direct computation
.—
Given the intricacy of our second-order expressions for volume and area fluctuations in Eqs. (
3.63
) and (
3.66
), the direct computation of the gauge transformation of the perturbations
\delta V
and
\delta A
is a very laborious task. Beyond having to make many substitutions, the main complication is that we need to relate partial derivatives with respect to spacetime coordinates
x^{\mu}
to derivatives with respect to the observed quantities
z,\theta,\phi
. Instead of explicitly showing the consistency between the expectation and the direct computation, which would involve many equations, we will provide the critical relations used to prove the expected consistency so that the interested reader can reproduce the calculations.

•

Relations between partial derivatives:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z}=-\frac{1}{H_{z}}{\partial\over\partial\eta}+\frac{1}{H_{z}}n^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial x^{\alpha}}\equiv\frac{1}{H_{z}}\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}=\bar{r}_{z}\theta^{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}=\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,\phi^{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\alpha}}\,.\qquad

(4.56)

•

Identities with unit vectors

\displaystyle\left(\cot\theta+\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\right)\theta^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\phi^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2n^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\theta^{\alpha}=\phi^{\alpha}\cos\theta\,,\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\theta^{\alpha}=-n^{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle n^{\alpha}\sin\theta+\theta^{\alpha}\cos\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\phi^{\alpha}\,.

(4.57)

•

Relations involving partial derivatives with respect to Cartesian coordinates and spherical coordinates

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\parallel}n^{\alpha}+\mathcal{L}_{\theta}\theta^{\alpha}+\mathcal{L}_{\phi}\phi^{\alpha}\,,

(4.58)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\alpha}=\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}+\mathcal{L}_{\phi,\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\frac{1}{\bar{r}}_{z}\theta^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial\theta}\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta}\phi^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial\phi}\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}\,,

(4.59)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}{}_{,\alpha}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\alpha}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}{}_{,\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\parallel}\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}+\mathcal{L}_{\phi,\parallel}\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}+\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{\phi,\beta}\theta^{\beta}-\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\parallel}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}

(4.60)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{\parallel,\parallel}\mathcal{L}_{\phi,\alpha}\phi^{\alpha}-\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\alpha}\theta^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{\phi,\beta}\phi^{\beta}\,,

which also apply to
\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}
, or any other field.

•

Relations involving derivatives of spatial deviations

\displaystyle\Delta x^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{\alpha}\delta r+\theta^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\,\delta\theta+\phi^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,\delta\phi\,,

(4.61)

\displaystyle{\partial\over\partial z}\Delta x^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial z}\delta r+\frac{1}{H_{z}}\theta^{\alpha}\delta\theta+\theta^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}{\partial\over\partial z}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{H_{z}}\phi^{\alpha}\sin\theta\,\delta\phi+\phi^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta{\partial\over\partial z}\delta\phi\,,

(4.62)

\displaystyle{\partial\over\partial\theta}\Delta x^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial\theta}\delta r+\theta^{\alpha}\delta r+\bar{r}_{z}\delta\theta{\partial\over\partial\theta}\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}{\partial\over\partial\theta}\delta\theta+\phi^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\cos\theta\,\delta\phi+\phi^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta{\partial\over\partial\theta}\delta\phi\,,\qquad\,\,\,

(4.63)

\displaystyle{\partial\over\partial\phi}\Delta x^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{\alpha}{\partial\over\partial\phi}\delta r+\phi^{\alpha}\sin\theta\,\delta r+\bar{r}_{z}\delta\theta{\partial\over\partial\phi}\theta^{\alpha}+\theta^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}{\partial\over\partial\phi}\delta\theta+\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta\,\delta\phi{\partial\over\partial\phi}\phi^{\alpha}

(4.64)

\displaystyle+\phi^{\alpha}\bar{r}_{z}\sin\theta{\partial\over\partial\phi}\delta\phi\,.

Employing these relations, and directly substituting in Eqs. (
3.63
) and (
3.66
) the gauge-transformation laws derived previously in the section we obtain

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta V}}(\widetilde{x}_{s})={\delta V}(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\left({\partial\over\partial x^{\mu}}\delta V\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,\qquad{\widetilde{\delta A}}(\widetilde{x}_{s})={\delta A}(\bar{x}_{z})+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}\left({\partial\over\partial x^{\mu}}\delta A\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,,\quad

(4.65)

in agreement with the expectation in Eq. (
4.54
) once the right-hand side is expanded up to second order around
\bar{x}^{\mu}_{z}
.

5
Comparison with Previous Works

In this section, we compare our main results with previous work in the literature. The physical volume and area, as well as the observed redshift and source position, have already been computed at the second order in perturbations, and many applications have been proposed
[
68
,
69
,
70
,
71
,
72
,
73
,
74
,
77
,
78
,
79
,
80
,
81
,
82
,
83
,
84
,
85
,
86
,
87
,
88
,
89
,
90
,
91
,
92
,
93
,
94
,
95
,
96
,
97
,
98
,
99
,
100
,
101
,
102
]
.
However, in none of these previous works have the gauge transformation properties of the main results been explicitly verified. Given the complex nature of the second-order relativistic expressions of the cosmological observables, complete verification of the gauge-transformation properties in our work provides strong support for the validity of our results that are likely to have less (or no) flaws. Due to the different notations and intricacy of the
expressions, it is not straightforward to make a detailed comparison between our results and those of other groups. Hence, we can only conduct a qualitative comparison at this stage, and any quantitative comparison is reserved for future works.
Therefore, we will list previous efforts in literature, highlighting only the main differences with our results.

•

Yoo, J., & Zaldarriaga, M. (2014). Beyond the linear-order relativistic effect in galaxy clustering: second-order gauge-invariant formalism
[
93
]
.

This article can be regarded as a prior work of what we have set out here. In fact, except for a few minor details and notation conventions, the approach and the methodology coincide but here we focus more on the exact nonlinear relations and definitions, separating perturbative expressions. In
[
93
]
, the general relativistic description of galaxy clustering was extended to the second order in metric perturbations without assuming any gauge conditions or adopting any restrictions on vector and tensor perturbations. Moreover, second-order gauge-invariant formalism was discussed, computing second-order gauge-invariant variables.
However, no explicit check of gauge transformation was made, over which we improve here. Furthermore, with the recent development of the tetrad formalism
[
63
]
, we have clarified the relation of the physical observables to coordinate-dependent quantities.

•

Di Dio, E., Durrer, R., Marozzi, G., & Montanari, F. (2014). Galaxy number counts to second order and their bispectrum
[
94
]
.

The authors present the galaxy number counts to the second order in perturbations
and compute the angular bispectrum. They identify the dominant contributions
to the angular bispectrum of the galaxy number counts,
which originate from the density fluctuation, the redshift space distortion, and
gravitational lensing. Using the geodesic light-cone coordinate
[
111
,
112
]
, the authors
derive the nonlinear expressions for the cosmological observables without solving
for the Einstein equation (see, e.g.,
[
113
]
). Then they transform their expressions to
the second-order Poisson gauge, neglecting vector and tensor contributions
to the metric perturbations but without assuming vanishing anisotropic stress.
Their expression in Eq. (3.23) shows the relation of perturbation variables
to the observed redshift:

\displaystyle 1+z_{s}=\frac{a(\eta_{o})}{a(\eta_{s})}\left[1+\delta z^{(1)}+\delta z^{(2)}\right]\,,

(5.1)

which is different from ours given in Eq. (
2.52
), but we stress that the
difference arises only from a different choice of splitting the background and the
perturbation. However, their explicit expression in Eq. (3.23) in terms of metric
perturbations contains only the contributions evaluated at the source position and
along the-line-of-sight direction, while no contributions at the observer position
are present. This equation can be compared to Eq. (
B.3
) in our work, where
there exist not only the contributions at the observer position, but also
the quadratic contributions that involve one at the observer position and the
other at the source position. Similar differences are present in their expressions
for the angular position in Eq. (3.15), which can be compared to Eq. (
3.54
) in our
work. These differences persist in their subsequent expressions based on the source
position such as the fluctuation in the physical volume and the area.

•

D. Bertacca, R. Maartens, and C. Clarkson. Observed galaxy number counts on the light cone up
to second order: II. Derivation
[
96
]
.

This paper aims to develop the second-order framework for studying
the relativistic effects beyond the linear order, in the same spirit
of our current work. The final results are presented both in the Poisson gauge and
in a general metric representation without assuming a gauge condition,
but the vector and tensor perturbations at the linear order are ignored.
Due to the different notation convention, it is not easy to conduct
a detailed comparison, but the calculational steps are quite similar to ours.
In
[
96
]
(BMC hence forth) they use the comoving distance as an affine parameter
\chi
, which corresponds
to our affine parameter
-\lambda
with negative sign and gives rise to
the sign difference in the conformal wave vector. The perturbative deviation of their affine parameter
\chi
around the background comoving distance
\bar{\chi}
is equivalent to ours in Eq. (
2.59
).
The main difference lies in their notation convention for the conformal wave vector, which is given in their Eq. (11):

\displaystyle k^{\mu}(\bar{\chi})=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\bar{\chi}}(\bar{\chi})=\left(-1+\delta\nu,\,n^{i}+{\delta n}^{i}\right)^{\mu}(\bar{\chi})\,.

(5.2)

The corresponding quantity
\hat{k}^{\mu}
in our notation is defined in Eq. (
2.25
) and it their notation is

\displaystyle k^{\mu}(\chi)=\frac{dx^{\mu}}{d\chi}(\chi)=\left(-1-\delta\nu,\,n^{i}+{\delta n}^{i}\right)^{\mu}(\chi)\,.

(5.3)

Therefore, what we call
\delta\nu
and
{\delta n}^{i}
in our papers are different quantities, where the difference amounts to another choice of the parameterization of the light path.
For instance, the difference in what we call
\delta\nu
is given by

\displaystyle\delta\nu_{MY}=-\delta\nu_{BMC}-\left(1-\delta\nu_{BMC}\right)\frac{d}{d\chi}\delta\chi\,.

(5.4)

Notice also that their geodesic equation for the conformal light wave vector above Eq. (86) looks different from what we use to derive Eqs (
3.35
) and (
3.36
), namely

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\lambda}\hat{k}^{\mu}+{\hat{\Gamma}}^{\mu}_{\nu\rho}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\lambda}}\hat{k}^{\nu}\hat{k}^{\rho}+\delta x^{\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}{\hat{\Gamma}}^{\mu}_{\nu\rho}\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\lambda}}\hat{k}^{\nu}\hat{k}^{\rho}=0\,.

(5.5)

However, further expanding this equation around
\bar{x}(\lambda_{z})
and
\lambda_{z}
, and using the linear-order relation

\displaystyle\Delta x^{\mu}=\delta x^{\mu}+\Delta\lambda_{z}\hat{\bar{k}}^{\mu}\,,

(5.6)

we find consistency with their expression.
The use of
\bar{\chi}
instead of
\chi
yields their definition of
\delta x^{0}
and
\delta x^{i}
in Eqs. (16) and (17), which amounts to
adopting the Born approximation. However, the authors do include post-Born terms (see for instance their Eq. (113)) which make
a difference at the second order in which we have to account for the evolution
of perturbations on a perturbed light path. The observed redshift is defined in their Eq. (27), and the normalization condition for the conformal wave vector in their Eq. (28) corresponds to our
condition in Eq. (
2.28
). However, adopting
a(\eta_{o})=1
, they ignored
the contributions at the observer position. Indeed, as apparent in our Eq. (
2.46
)
the observer position in an inhomogeneous universe is not uniquely set, but stochastically
set in a given realization of the inhomogeneous universe. With such normalization, all
the background equations with
a(\bar{\eta}_{\rm o})
, which are mostly not shown explicitly,
result in extra perturbative quantities, because of the normalization condition.
Similarly, the spatial shift of the observer position
\delta x^{\alpha}_{o}
in our Eq. (
3.14
)
is ignored, which matters starting at the second order in perturbations.
Finally, the antisymmetric part of the tetrad vectors in our Eq. (
3.32
) is not
considered in BMC, which again matters in the second-order expressions for the
cosmological observations.
No verification of the gauge-transformation properties of their expressions are made.

•

O. Umeh, C. Clarkson, and Roy Maartens. Nonlinear relativistic corrections to cosmological
distances, redshift and gravitational lensing magnification: II. Derivation
[
87
]
.

This paper studies light propagation in a generic spacetime to derive a nonlinear expression for the area distance. This is computed up to second order in perturbations in the Poisson gauge, and their results are expressed using the observed redshift as parameterization.
It is not straightforward to directly compare our results for the area distance with those obtained by the authors. These expressions are lengthy, the notation conventions are different, and the authors fix a gauge. For these reasons, we will compare the calculations in the intermediate steps rather than the final but lengthy result (see also
[
89
,
109
]
for similar
derivations based on the geodesic light cone gauge that include the contributions
at the observer position).
The authors consider two spacetimes: quantities defined on the physical spacetime are denoted by a hat, while quantities defined on a conformally related manifold have no hat. They specify the conformal transformation in their Eq. (21), where the only difference with our derivation is that they fix the constant
\mathbb{C}=1
. A consequence of this choice is that quantities like the light wave vector or the perturbation in the observed redshift are different from our expressions. However, this should not impact the final expressions for the cosmological observables. Another difference between the two approaches is that the authors fix
\delta k^{\mu}_{o}=0
in the light wave vector at the observer position, both at first and second order. In our case, such quantity satisfies the boundary conditions in Eq. (
2.32
) involving tetrad vectors at the observer. Such contributions at the observer position appear in our final result for the area distance but are neglected in the paper we are discussing. Moreover, in their definition of the redshift perturbation in Eq. (69), they adopt
a(\eta_{o})=1
, ignoring contributions at the observer position.
Notice that the authors obtain their expression for the angular diameter distance by solving the differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}\delta^{n}D_{A}}{d\lambda^{2}}=\delta^{n}S(\lambda)\,,

(5.7)

with boundary conditions

\displaystyle\delta^{n}D_{A}(\lambda_{o})=0\,,\qquad\qquad\frac{d\delta^{n}D_{A}}{d\lambda}\bigg\rvert_{o}=-\delta^{n}E_{o}\,,

(5.8)

where
\delta^{n}S
contains the area distance up to order
n-1
and, in our notation,
E_{0}=-(\hat{u}^{\mu}\hat{k}_{\mu})_{o}=1+\widehat{\Delta\nu}_{o}
. Given our choice of the constant
\mathbb{C}
we fixed
\widehat{\Delta\nu}_{o}=0
while for the authors
E_{o}
contain also a perturbative part. As a consequence, the boundary condition in Eq. (11) are different in the two approaches, hence also the solution for the angular diameter distance will be different.
There is another difference present for the relation between the affine parameter and the observed redshift. In their Eq. (126)

\displaystyle\lambda=\nu+\delta\lambda+\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\lambda\,,

(5.9)

where the definition of
\lambda
coincides with our
\lambda_{s}
, while
\nu
coincides with our
\bar{\eta}_{z}
. In Eqs. (132) and (133) they derive their expressions for
\delta\lambda
and
\delta^{2}\lambda
. Such expressions coincide with what we derived for
\Delta\eta_{s}
in Eq. (
3.51
). So in our notation we could rewrite Eq. (12) as

\displaystyle\lambda_{s}=\bar{\eta}_{z}+\Delta\eta_{s}\,,

(5.10)

which actually differs from our expression in Eq. (
2.59
) of the terms
-\bar{\eta}_{\rm o}-\delta\eta_{s}
and would then impact all the expansions in the mapping from affine parameter to observed redshift.

6
Summary and Discussion

In this paper we have developed the nonlinear gauge-invariant formalism for describing the cosmological observables and presented complete second-order expressions associated with light propagation and observations without choosing a gauge condition. Our second-order expressions in general-metric representations allow us to explicitly check the validity by directly computing their gauge transformations and by comparing to the expectations. Our main results are summarized as follows:

•

Adopting a geometric approach to describing the light propagation and observations, we have developed the nonlinear gauge-invariant formalism that is fundamental to computing all the general relativistic effects in cosmological observables. In particular, we have clarified the separation between the background and the perturbation quantities in a fully nonlinear way. Drawing on the previous work, we have introduced
tetrad vectors at the observer and the source positions to describe the observations recorded in the rest frame of the observer and to define the physical quantities in the source rest frame that are measured by the observer. The observable quantities are the redshift and the angular position of the source and their spectroscopic information; the physical quantities are the physical size and shape of the source and their spectrum in the rest
frame. These quantities are connected by light propagation and observations, and expressed in terms of intermediate quantities such as the coordinate position of the source or the distortion in the observed redshift. We have presented fully nonlinear expressions for the physical volume and area occupied by the source that can be measured in galaxy clustering and the luminosity distance. For future work we have left a nonlinear computation of the distortion of the source shape that can be measured in weak gravitational lensing.

•

By expanding the nonlinear expressions, we have computed the second-order expressions for the cosmological observables and their intermediate quantities in terms of metric perturbations and the velocities of the source and the observer. Any nonlinear quantities can be perturbatively expanded and truncated in many different ways, though the resulting expressions should be all consistent, if the perturbation order is defined in the same way. Paying particular attention to these subtleties, we have expanded the second-order quantities along the
background photon path and around the coordinate-independent reference points of the observer and the source parameterized by the observables. The reason behind this expansion is that first we can keep perturbative orders under control by expanding each quantity around background paths. More importantly, by having employed coordinate-independent parameterization for physical positions, the entire coordinate-dependence is contained in the deviations
\delta x^{\mu}_{o}
and
\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}
. The main results are the second-order gauge-invariant expressions of the physical volume and area for galaxy clustering and the luminosity distance.

•

Utilizing the geometric nature and nonlinear relations, we have first derived the expected second-order gauge-transformation properties of our theoretical descriptions of the observable quantities and the intermediate quantities. With the theoretical descriptions in a general metric representation, we have next performed the second-order gauge-transformation of the metric perturbation variables in those theoretical descriptions. By comparing the direct gauge-transformation properties to the expectations from the nonlinear relations, we have
explicitly verified
the consistency of our theoretical expressions, among which only the expressions for the physical volume and area occupied by the source are gauge-invariant
[
110
,
103
]
.

•

We have compared our results to those in the previous work and discussed the physical ingredients that are missed or ignored in the previous work.
7
7

7

As the current work was near completion, a work by
[
114
]
appeared on arXiv in which they present a nonlinear framework for computing the cosmological observables, but no second-order expressions or verification of gauge invariance are presented.
Among many differences, we have highlighted that the contributions at the observer position are typically neglected in literature and as a consequence all the quadratic terms that couple one contribution at the observer position and another contribution at the second order are also ignored. With any contributions missing, the second-order expressions in literature are incomplete and gauge-dependent.

Given the technical difficulties and theoretical subtleties in the second-order relativistic perturbation calculations of the cosmological observables, the second-order expressions in the previous work (including one of our own)
are generally considered
incomplete
with a few or several missing relativistic contributions. The explicit check of second-order gauge-invariance of the theoretical descriptions that we have performed
for the first time
constitutes a major step forward in establishing the
complete
second-order theoretical descriptions of the cosmological observables. With full degrees of freedom intact, our second-order expressions can be used to describe the cosmological observables in any theories of metric gravity. In the future, we will apply our second-order gauge-invariant formalism to compute the three-point correlation function of cosmological observables such as galaxy clustering, the luminosity distance, weak gravitational lensing, and cosmic microwave background anisotropies. Given the interest in the primordial non-Gaussianity, our complete second-order relativistic expressions will play a crucial role in providing accurate predictions for the higher-order statistics.

Appendix A
Gauge transformation of the light wave vector at the observer position

Here we present the consistency check for the components of the light wave vector
\hat{k}^{\mu}_{o}
at the observer position.
We start by computing the second-order expectation for the gauge transformations of
\delta\nu_{o}
and
{\delta n}^{\alpha}_{o}
. Taking the limit for
P\to O
of Eq. (
4.27
) and (
4.28
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta}\nu}_{o}:={\widetilde{\delta}\nu}(\widetilde{x}=x_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\nu_{o}+\bigg[-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T+T\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T^{\prime}+\mathcal{H}T\left(1+\delta\nu-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T\right)

(A.1)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\left(\delta\nu_{,\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}T_{,\alpha}-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T_{,\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}-3\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}-2\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}

\displaystyle+T^{\prime}\delta\nu-T_{,\alpha}{\delta n}^{\alpha}-T\delta\nu^{\prime}\bigg]_{o}\,,

\displaystyle{\widetilde{{\delta n}}}{}^{\alpha}_{o}:={\widetilde{{\delta n}}}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{x}=x_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\delta n}^{\alpha}_{o}+\bigg[\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}-T\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}+\mathcal{H}T\left(n^{\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\right)

(A.2)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\left({\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+2\mathcal{H}T_{,\beta}n^{\alpha}+\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}-3\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}n^{\alpha}

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}n^{\alpha}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}\delta\nu+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}{\delta n}^{\beta}-T{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}\bigg]_{o}\,.

We emphasize that both sides of the equations are evaluated at the coordinate value
x^{\mu}_{o}
, while the coordinates of the reference position
O
are different in the two charts.
An independent way to derive the gauge transformation of
\delta\nu_{o}
can be obtained considering the perturbation
\widehat{\Delta\nu}
. Let us rewrite the nonlinear expression in Eq. (
2.54
) in the new coordinate system denoted by a tilde:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\widehat{\Delta\nu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\parallel}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+(1+2\widetilde{\mathcal{A}})\left(\widetilde{\delta}u+\widetilde{\delta}\nu+\widetilde{\delta}u\,\widetilde{\delta}\nu\right)+\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\alpha}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}\right)+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}(1+\widetilde{\delta}\nu)

(A.3)

\displaystyle-\widetilde{\delta}u(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\delta n}_{\alpha})+2\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\parallel}+\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\alpha})\,,

where both sides are evaluated at the coordinates
\widetilde{x}_{p}^{\mu}
of a generic point
P
on the light path. Given our choice for the normalization constant in Eq. (
2.28
) and the relation (
2.29
), we readily see that the evaluation at
P=O
implies
\widetilde{\widehat{\Delta\nu}}(\widetilde{x}_{o})=0
. Hence, we can solve the equation above for the perturbation
\widetilde{\delta\nu}(\widetilde{x}_{o}
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}\nu(\widetilde{x}_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg[2\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\parallel}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+\widetilde{\delta}u+\widetilde{\delta}u\,\widetilde{\delta}\nu+2\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\left(\widetilde{\delta}u+\widetilde{\delta}\nu+\widetilde{\delta}u\,\widetilde{\delta}\nu\right)+\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\alpha}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}\right)

(A.4)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}(1+\widetilde{\delta}\nu)-\widetilde{\delta}u(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\delta n}_{\alpha})+2\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\parallel}+\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\beta}\widetilde{\delta n}{}^{\alpha})\bigg](\widetilde{x}_{o})\,.

We are interested in the evaluation at
x^{\mu}_{o}
since these coordinates correspond to the observer position in the original chart. Therefore, we expand the coordinates
\widetilde{x}^{\mu}_{o}
around the original coordinates
x^{\mu}_{o}
according to the coordinate transformation
\widetilde{x}^{\mu}(x)
in Eq. (
2.91
). At second order we obtain

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta}\nu}(\widetilde{x}=x_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg[\xi^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(\delta\nu+\mathcal{H}T+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}+{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}_{\parallel}-{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}}+\widetilde{\delta}\nu\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{2}

(A.5)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+\widetilde{\delta n}_{\alpha}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}+2\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\parallel}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}\bigg](\widetilde{x}=x_{o})\,,

where we used the linear-order gauge transformation of
\delta\nu
. Employing the linear-order definition of
\widehat{\Delta\nu}
from Eq. (
2.54
), we can see that the above equation yields

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta}\nu}(x_{o})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg[\xi^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(\widehat{\Delta\nu}+\mathcal{H}T\right)+{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}+{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}}_{\parallel}-{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}}+\widetilde{\delta}\nu\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{2}

(A.6)

\displaystyle+\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel}+\widetilde{\delta n}_{\alpha}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}+2\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha\parallel}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\alpha}\bigg](x_{o})\,,

Replacing all the gauge transformations with the results of Section
4
we finally derive

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\delta}\nu}_{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\nu_{o}+\bigg[-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T+T\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T^{\prime}+\mathcal{H}T\left(1+\delta\nu-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T\right)-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\left(\delta\nu_{,\alpha}+2\mathcal{H}T_{,\alpha}-\frac{d}{d\bar{r}}T_{,\alpha}\right)

(A.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}-3\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\right)T^{2}-2\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}+T^{\prime}\delta\nu-T_{,\alpha}{\delta n}^{\alpha}-T\delta\nu^{\prime}\bigg]_{o}\,,

which agrees with the previous computation in Eq. (
A.1
).

We now want to check that our second-order expression for
\hat{k}^{\mu}_{o}
gauge transforms consistently. Note that we have a second-order expression for
\delta\nu_{o}
in Eq. (
3.38
), but we do not have a complete second-order expression for
{\delta n}^{\alpha}_{o}
(see Eq. (
3.39
)). However, its relevant contribution to second-order is given by
S_{ij}
in Eq. (
3.4
), so we only need to prove the consistency of such expression.

To derive the second-order expectations we consider that at any spacetime point
x^{\mu}
the tetrad components
[e_{a}]^{\mu}
transform as a four-vector under diffeomorphisms of
\mathcal{M}
. The corresponding gauge transformation can be read from Eq. (
4
). Using the definition in Eq. (
2.12
) for the space-like tetrad components, we obtain at second order:

\displaystyle\widetilde{[e_{i}]}{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[e_{i}]^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{a}\bigg\{\delta^{\alpha}_{i}(\mathcal{H}T)+\delta^{\beta}_{i}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\delta^{\beta}_{i}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}(\mathcal{U}_{\beta}-\mathcal{B}_{\beta})-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\left[\delta^{\gamma}_{i}(\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{\gamma}+C^{[\beta}{}_{,\gamma]})+\epsilon^{\beta}{}_{ik}\Omega^{k}\right]

(A.8)

\displaystyle+\big(T\partial_{\eta}+\mathcal{L}^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}-\mathcal{H}T\big)\left[\delta^{\gamma}_{i}(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\gamma}+C^{[\alpha}{}_{,\gamma]})+\epsilon^{\alpha}{}_{ik}\Omega^{k}\right]-\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}-\mathcal{H}\mathcal{L}^{\beta}T_{,\beta}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}-\mathcal{H}^{2})T^{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}-T\mathcal{L}^{\prime\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\delta^{\beta}_{i}-\mathcal{L}^{\gamma}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta\gamma}\delta^{\beta}_{i}+\mathcal{H}T\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\delta^{\beta}_{i}\bigg\}\,,

where both sides are evaluated at the same coordinate value
x_{o}^{\mu}
. Taking the symmetric and antisymmetric part of the last equation and contracting the Kronecker deltas, we derive

\displaystyle{\widetilde{S}}_{ij}-S_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\delta_{ij}\mathcal{H}T-\mathcal{L}_{(i,j)}+\mathcal{B}_{(j}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i)}-\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{ij}T+\mathcal{H}T\mathcal{C}_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ij}\mathcal{H}^{2}T^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ij}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}T^{2}

(A.9)

\displaystyle+\delta_{ij}\mathcal{H}TT^{\prime}+\mathcal{L}_{(i,\alpha}\varepsilon_{j)k}{}^{\alpha}\Omega^{k}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(i}\mathcal{U}_{j)}-\mathcal{H}T\mathcal{L}_{(i,j)}+T\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{(i,j)}-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\mathcal{C}_{ij,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}C^{\alpha}{}_{,(i}\mathcal{L}_{j),\alpha}+\delta_{ij}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}T_{,\alpha}+\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{(i,j)\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{(i,\alpha}C_{j)}{}^{,\alpha}+\mathcal{C}_{\alpha(i}\mathcal{L}_{j)}{}^{,\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{ij}-A_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{[i,j]}+\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{[i}\left(\mathcal{B}_{j]}-\mathcal{U}_{j]}\right)-T\left(C^{\prime}_{[i,j]}-\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{[i,j]}+\varepsilon_{ijk}\Omega^{\prime k}\right)

(A.10)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{H}T\left(C_{[i,j]}-\mathcal{L}_{[i,j]}+\varepsilon_{ijk}\Omega^{k}\right)-\mathcal{L}^{\alpha}\left(C_{[i,j]\alpha}-\mathcal{L}_{[i,j]\alpha}+\varepsilon_{ijl}\Omega^{l}{}_{,\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{[i}{}^{,\alpha}\left(2\mathcal{C}_{j]\alpha}+C_{\alpha,j]}-C_{j],\alpha}-2\varepsilon_{j]\alpha l}\Omega^{l}\right)\,,

according to the definition of the split in Eq. (
2.15
). From the gauge transformation
\widetilde{A}_{ij}
we can infer the linear-order expression of
A_{ij}
in Eq. (
3.32
) which indeed transforms accordingly considering that
\Omega^{i}
is gauge-invariant and
C_{\alpha}
at linear order gauge transforms as the vector part of
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\beta}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{\alpha}=C_{\alpha}-L_{\alpha}\,.

(A.11)

Finally, directly substituting all the gauge transformations in our second-order expression for
S_{ij}
in Eq. (
3.4
), we recover Eq. (
A.9
), proving the validity of our expression.

Appendix B
Explicit expressions of the most relevant second-order quantities

In Section
3
we derived all the second-order expressions related to the light propagation from a luminous source to the observer position. In Table
B
we provide the references to such equations.

Of all these quantities, the most relevant are those related to cosmological observables, i.e., the redshift fluctuation
\delta z
, the distortion in the source position
\delta r,\delta\theta,\delta\phi
, and the perturbation in the physical volume
\delta V
and area
\delta A
.

We now present the second-order expressions of these quantities in terms of metric perturbations and observer velocity. For clarity we will keep some of those quantities in the table at the linear order (i.e., they will not be expressed in terms of the metric), but we provide the reference to their expressions.
Note that the second-order antisymmetric part
A^{j}{}_{i}
of the space-like tetrad perturbation
\delta e^{\alpha}_{i}
never appears.

Distortion in the source position
(\delta r,\delta\theta,\delta\phi)
in Eq. (
3.6
)

\displaystyle\delta r

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bar{r}_{z}\Bigg[\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+n^{i}n_{j}\delta^{j}_{\alpha}\bigg(A^{\alpha}{}_{i}+\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{i}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{i}\Omega^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}\Omega^{k}\Omega_{k}-\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{i}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\beta}

(B.1)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k\beta}\Omega^{k}-\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta,(i}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha)\beta}+\frac{1}{2}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k\beta}\Omega^{k}-\frac{1}{2}C_{(i,\beta}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k}{}^{\beta}\Omega^{k}+\frac{1}{8}C^{\beta}{}_{,i}C_{\beta}{}^{,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}C_{i,}{}^{\beta}C^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}-\frac{1}{4}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}\bigg)-\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}

\displaystyle-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-2\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)C_{[\alpha,\parallel]}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{\alpha ij}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)n^{i}\Omega^{j}\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+n_{\alpha}\delta x_{o}^{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}\delta x^{\alpha})\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+n_{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\bar{t}_{\rm o}}dt\,\frac{1}{a}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{A}\,\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{U}^{\beta}\delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{t}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\Bigg[\bigg(-\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-2\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)C_{[\alpha,\parallel]}+\varepsilon_{\alpha ij}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)n^{i}\Omega^{j}\bigg)_{\bar{\rm o}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\bigg[\delta\nu\delta\nu^{\prime}-{\delta n}^{\alpha}\delta\nu_{,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\prime}+2\delta\nu\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)-2\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-2{\delta n}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{(\alpha,\parallel)}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}^{\prime}\right)-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha,\parallel]}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\alpha}\right)\bigg]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\delta\nu\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+{\delta n}_{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\bar{\rm o}}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\bar{\rm o}}\right)\delta\eta_{o}^{2}+\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\delta\eta_{o}\widehat{\Delta\nu}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}(\partial_{\mu}\widehat{\Delta\nu})\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\Bigg]+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathcal{H}^{2}_{z}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{z}}{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\left(\frac{\delta z}{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\Bigg(\frac{\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}}{\mathcal{H}_{z}}-1\Bigg)\Bigg[\delta x^{\mu}_{o}(\partial_{\mu}\delta t)\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{t}_{\rm o}}dt\left(-\mathcal{A}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{a}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\delta t_{,\alpha}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{t}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\delta t_{o}^{2}+n_{\alpha}\left[\Delta x_{\bar{r}}^{\mu}\,\partial_{\mu}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta\right)\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\right]^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}+n_{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\Bigg[-{\delta n}^{\beta}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}\right)

\displaystyle+\delta\nu\left(\Delta x^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta^{\prime}\right)-\left(\bar{r}_{z}-\bar{r}\right)\bigg(-\delta\nu{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\;\;\;\;,\alpha}+\delta\nu\left(2\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-2\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-{\delta n}^{\gamma}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\;\;,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\gamma}+2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\gamma}-4\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{(\parallel,\gamma)}+2\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\gamma}^{\;\;\;\;,\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{C}_{\gamma\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\gamma}+\mathcal{A}_{,\gamma}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma}-2\mathcal{B}_{[\parallel,\gamma]}-2\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\gamma}^{\prime}\right)+n^{\alpha}\bigg(\delta\nu\delta\nu^{\prime}-{\delta n}^{\alpha}\delta\nu_{,\alpha}+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel,\parallel}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\prime}+2\delta\nu\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)-2\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)-2{\delta n}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{(\alpha,\parallel)}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha,\parallel]}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\alpha}\right)\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}+\frac{\bar{r}_{z}}{2}\left(\delta\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta~\delta\phi^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\delta\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{r}}_{z}\theta_{\alpha}\Bigg\{-\bar{r}_{z}\Bigg[\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+n^{i}\bigg(A^{\alpha}{}_{i}+\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{i}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{i}\Omega^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}\Omega^{k}\Omega_{k}-\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{i}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\beta}

(B.2)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k\beta}\Omega^{k}-\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta,(i}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha)\beta}+\frac{1}{2}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k\beta}\Omega^{k}-\frac{1}{2}C_{(i,\beta}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k}{}^{\beta}\Omega^{k}+\frac{1}{8}C^{\beta}{}_{,i}C_{\beta}{}^{,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}C_{i,}{}^{\beta}C^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}-\frac{1}{4}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+\delta x_{o}^{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}\delta x^{\alpha})\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+\left[\Delta x_{\bar{r}}^{\mu}\,\partial_{\mu}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta\right)\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\right]^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\bar{t}_{\rm o}}dt\,\frac{1}{a}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{A}\,\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{U}^{\beta}\delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{t}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\Bigg[-{\delta n}^{\beta}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}\right)

\displaystyle+\delta\nu\left(\Delta x^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta^{\prime}\right)-\left(\bar{r}_{z}-\bar{r}\right)\bigg(-\delta\nu{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\;\;\;\;,\alpha}+\delta\nu\left(2\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-2\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-{\delta n}^{\gamma}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}^{\;\;,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\gamma}+2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\gamma}-4\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{(\parallel,\gamma)}+2\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\gamma}^{\;\;\;\;,\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{C}_{\gamma\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\gamma}+\mathcal{A}_{,\gamma}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma}-2\mathcal{B}_{[\parallel,\gamma]}-2\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\gamma}^{\prime}\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\Bigg\}-\frac{\delta r}{\bar{r}_{z}}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta~\delta\phi^{2}\,.

The explicit expressions of the linear-order terms are given in Eqs. (

\displaystyle+\bigg(-\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-2\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)C_{[\alpha,\parallel]}+\varepsilon_{\alpha ij}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)n^{i}\Omega^{j}\bigg)_{\bar{\rm o}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\bigg[\delta\nu\delta\nu^{\prime}-{\delta n}^{\alpha}\delta\nu_{,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\delta\nu\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+{\delta n}_{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\bar{\rm o}}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\bar{\rm o}}\right)\delta\eta_{o}^{2}+\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\delta\eta_{o}\widehat{\Delta\nu}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}(\partial_{\mu}\widehat{\Delta\nu})\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\,.

Perturbation in the physical volume
\delta V
occupied by the source in Eq. (

)

\displaystyle\delta V

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\Bigg[\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\Bigg(\delta x^{\mu}_{o}(\partial_{\mu}\delta t)\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\delta t_{o}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\bar{t}_{\rm o}}dt\left(-\mathcal{A}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{a}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\delta t_{,\alpha}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{t}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(-\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-2\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)C_{[\alpha,\parallel]}+\varepsilon_{\alpha ij}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)n^{i}\Omega^{j}\bigg)_{\bar{\rm o}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\bigg[\delta\nu\delta\nu^{\prime}-{\delta n}^{\alpha}\delta\nu_{,\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\prime}+2\delta\nu\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)-2\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-2{\delta n}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{(\alpha,\parallel)}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}^{\prime}\right)-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha,\parallel]}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\alpha}\right)\bigg]\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\delta\nu\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+{\delta n}_{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{H}^{2}_{\bar{\rm o}}+\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\bar{\rm o}}\right)\delta\eta_{o}^{2}+\mathcal{H}_{\bar{\rm o}}\delta\eta_{o}\widehat{\Delta\nu}+\Delta x^{\mu}_{s}(\partial_{\mu}\widehat{\Delta\nu})\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{z}}\Bigg]+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\alpha}-{1\over 2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}+\mathcal{A}~\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+{1\over 2}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\alpha}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{\beta}-\mathcal{C}^{\alpha\beta}\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\beta}+H_{z}\frac{\bar{r}_{z}}{2}{\partial\over\partial z}\left(\delta\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta~\delta\phi^{2}\right)+\frac{3}{2}\left(\delta\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta~\delta\phi^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-2{\delta n}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{(\alpha,\parallel)}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}^{\prime}\right)-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha,\parallel]}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\alpha}\right)\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\left(\cot\theta+{\partial\over\partial\theta}\right)\Biggr(\frac{1}{\bar{r}}_{z}\theta_{\alpha}\Bigg\{-\bar{r}_{z}\Bigg[\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+n^{i}\bigg(A^{\alpha}{}_{i}+\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{i}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{i}\Omega^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}\Omega^{k}\Omega_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}C_{i,}{}^{\beta}C^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}-\frac{1}{4}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}\bigg)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+\delta x_{o}^{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}\delta x^{\alpha})\big\rvert_{\bar{\rm o}}+\left[\Delta x_{\bar{r}}^{\mu}\,\partial_{\mu}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta\right)\big\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\right]^{z}_{\bar{\rm o}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\bar{t}_{\rm o}}dt\,\frac{1}{a}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{A}\,\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{U}^{\beta}\delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}\right)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{t}}+\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\Bigg[-{\delta n}^{\beta}\left(\Delta x^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta_{,\beta}\right)

\displaystyle+\delta\nu\left(\Delta x^{\prime\alpha}+n^{\alpha}\Delta\eta^{\prime}\right)-\left(\bar{r}_{z}-\bar{r}\right)\bigg(-\delta\nu{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha\gamma}\left(2\mathcal{C}_{\gamma\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\gamma}+\mathcal{A}_{,\gamma}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma}-2\mathcal{B}_{[\parallel,\gamma]}-2\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\gamma}^{\prime}\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}\Bigg\}\Biggr)

\displaystyle-n_{\alpha}\bigg(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+A^{\alpha}{}_{i}+\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{i}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{i}\Omega^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\delta^{\alpha}_{i}\Omega^{k}\Omega_{k}-\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{i}\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\beta}-\mathcal{C}^{\beta}_{(i}\varepsilon^{\alpha)}{}_{k\beta}\Omega^{k}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}C^{\beta}{}_{,(i}C^{\alpha)}{}_{,\beta}\bigg)_{\bar{\rm o}}n^{i}-n_{\alpha}\int_{0}^{\bar{r}_{z}}d\bar{r}\bigg[-\delta\nu{\delta n}^{\prime\alpha}+{\delta n}^{\beta}{\delta n}^{\alpha}{}_{,\beta}+\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\;\;\;\;,\alpha}+\delta\nu\left(2\mathcal{A}^{,\alpha}-2\mathcal{B}^{\prime\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}^{\;\;,\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\;\;,\parallel}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime\alpha}_{\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\Bigg[\bigg(-\mathcal{A}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}+\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-2\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}\right)-\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+{\mathcal{B}}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}^{\prime}+2\delta\nu\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}\right)-2\mathcal{A}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}-2\mathcal{A}_{,\parallel}+\mathcal{B}_{\parallel,\parallel}+\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle-2{\delta n}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}_{(\alpha,\parallel)}-\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}^{\prime}\right)-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{A}_{,\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha,\parallel]}-2\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{\alpha\parallel}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel,\parallel}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel,\alpha}\right)\bigg)\bigg\rvert_{\bar{x}_{\bar{r}}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+\delta\nu\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}^{2}+\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}+{\delta n}_{\alpha}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}-\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}+2\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\parallel}\mathcal{U}^{\alpha}\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathcal{A}\left(-2\kappa+2\frac{\delta r}{\bar{r}_{z}}-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\parallel}-\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}\right)

\displaystyle+\widehat{\Delta\nu}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)+\delta\theta\left(\mathcal{U}_{\theta}+{\delta n}_{\theta}\right)-\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{z}}\left[\left(\mathcal{U}_{\theta}+{\delta n}_{\theta}\right){\partial\over\partial\theta}+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\phi}+{\delta n}_{\phi}\right){\partial\over\partial\phi}\right]\delta r

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{z}}\delta\theta{\partial\over\partial\theta}\delta r+\sin\theta\,\delta\phi\left(\mathcal{U}_{\phi}+{\delta n}_{\phi}\right)+\frac{1}{\bar{r}_{z}}\delta\phi{\partial\over\partial\phi}\delta r+\delta z^{2}+2\,\delta z\delta g

\displaystyle+\left(2\,\delta z+\delta g\right)\left(-\mathcal{A}+2\frac{\delta r}{\bar{r}_{z}}-2\kappa-\mathcal{C}_{\parallel\parallel}\right)+\Delta x^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\left(2\,\delta z+\delta g-\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{U}_{\parallel}+{\delta n}_{\parallel}-\widehat{\Delta\nu}\right)\,.\qquad\,\,\,
